Examples

One example per SDK, generated from the API model. The argument names are the parameters and fields of this entity, and the values are placeholders. The examples are not compiled or executed.

Golang

Construct client as shown in the Golang setup, then call the operations:

authorizations, err := client.Authorization(nil).List(nil, nil)
if err != nil {
    panic(err)
}
fmt.Println(authorizations)
authorization, err := client.Authorization(nil).Load(map[string]any{"id": "example_id"}, nil)
if err != nil {
    panic(err)
}
fmt.Println(authorization)
created, err := client.Authorization(nil).Create(map[string]any{"id": "example", "amount": 1, "approved": true, "authorization_method": "example", "balance_transactions": []any{}, "card": map[string]any{}, "created": 1, "currency": "example", "livemode": true, "merchant_amount": 1, "merchant_currency": "example", "merchant_data": map[string]any{}, "metadata": map[string]any{}, "object": "example", "request_history": []any{}, "status": "example", "transactions": []any{}, "verification_data": map[string]any{}}, nil)
if err != nil {
    panic(err)
}
fmt.Println(created)

Field Type Required Description
amount integer Yes The total amount that was authorized or rejected.
amount_details any No Detailed breakdown of amount components.
approved boolean Yes Whether the authorization has been approved.
authorization_method string Yes How the card details were provided.
balance_transactions array Yes List of balance transactions associated with this authorization.
card object Yes You can create physical or virtual cards that are issued to cardholders.
card_presence string No Whether the card was present at the point of sale for the authorization.
cardholder any No The cardholder to whom this authorization belongs.
created integer Yes Time at which the object was created.
currency string Yes The currency of the cardholder.
fleet any No Fleet-specific information for authorizations using Fleet cards.
fraud_challenges array No Fraud challenges sent to the cardholder, if this authorization was declined for fraud risk reasons.
fuel any No Information about fuel that was purchased with this transaction.
id string Yes Unique identifier for the object.
livemode boolean Yes If the object exists in live mode, the value is true.
merchant_amount integer Yes The total amount that was authorized or rejected.
merchant_currency string Yes The local currency that was presented to the cardholder for the authorization.
merchant_data object Yes Merchant Data
metadata object Yes Set of key-value pairs that you can attach to an object.
network_data any No Details about the authorization, such as identifiers, set by the card network.
object string Yes String representing the object's type.

request_history array Yes History of every time a pending_request authorization was approved/declined, either by you directly or by Stripe (for example
status string Yes The current status of the authorization in its lifecycle.
token string No Token object used for this authorization.
transactions array Yes List of transactions associated with this authorization.
treasury any No Treasury details related to this authorization if it was created on a FinancialAccount.
verification_data object Yes Verification Data
verified_by_fraud_challenge boolean No Whether the authorization bypassed fraud risk checks because the cardholder has previously completed a fraud challenge on a similar high-risk authorization from the same merchant.
wallet string No The digital wallet used for this transaction.

create

POST /v1/issuing/authorizations/{authorization}

Operation ID: PostIssuingAuthorizationsAuthorization.

Authentication: basic token (basicAuth) or bearer token (bearerAuth).

Parameters

| Parameter | In | Type | Required | Description | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | authorization | path | string | Yes | |

Request body

application/x-www-form-urlencoded

Property Type Required Description
expand array of string No Specifies which fields in the response should be expanded.
metadata object or enum: No Set of key-value pairs that you can attach to an object. This can be useful for storing additional information about the object in a structured format. Individual keys can be unset by posting an empty value to them. All keys can be unset by posting an empty value to metadata.

Responses

200: Successful response

application/json

Property Type Required Description
amount integer Yes The total amount that was authorized or rejected. This amount is in currency and in the smallest currency unit. amount should be the same as merchant_amount, unless currency and merchant_currency are different.
amount_details object No Detailed breakdown of amount components. These amounts are denominated in currency and in the smallest currency unit.
approved boolean Yes Whether the authorization has been approved.
authorization_method enum: chip, contactless, keyed_in, online, swipe Yes How the card details were provided.
balance_transactions array of object Yes List of balance transactions associated with this authorization.
card object Yes You can create physical or virtual cards that are issued to cardholders.
card.brand string Yes The brand of the card.
card.cancellation_reason enum: design_rejected, fulfillment_error, lost, stolen No The reason why the card was canceled.
card.cardholder object Yes An Issuing Cardholder object represents an individual or business entity who is issued cards. Related guide: How to create a cardholder
card.cardholder.billing object Yes IssuingCardholderAddress
card.cardholder.billing.address object Yes Address
card.cardholder.billing.address.city string No City, district, suburb, town, or village.
card.cardholder.billing.address.country string No Two-letter country code (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2).
card.cardholder.billing.address.line1 string No Address line 1, such as the street, PO Box, or company name.
card.cardholder.billing.address.line2 string No Address line 2, such as the apartment, suite, unit, or building.
card.cardholder.billing.address.postal_code string No ZIP or postal code.
card.cardholder.billing.address.state string No State, county, province, or region (ISO 3166-2).
card.cardholder.company object No Additional information about a company cardholder.
card.cardholder.created integer (unix-time) Yes Time at which the object was created. Measured in seconds since the Unix epoch.
card.cardholder.email string No The cardholder's email address.
card.cardholder.id string Yes Unique identifier for the object.
card.cardholder.individual object No Additional information about an individual cardholder.
card.cardholder.livemode boolean Yes If the object exists in live mode, the value is true. If the object exists in test mode, the value is false.
card.cardholder.metadata object Yes Set of key-value pairs that you can attach to an object. This can be useful for storing additional information about the object in a structured format.
card.cardholder.name string Yes The cardholder's name. This will be printed on cards issued to them.
card.cardholder.object enum: issuing.cardholder Yes String representing the object's type. Objects of the same type share the same value.
card.cardholder.phone_number string No The cardholder's phone number. This is required for all cardholders who will be creating EU cards. See the 3D Secure documentation for more details.
card.cardholder.preferred_locales array of enum: de, en, es, fr, it No The cardholder’s preferred locales (languages), ordered by preference. Locales can be de, en, es, fr, or it. This changes the language of the 3D Secure flow and one-time password messages sent to the cardholder.
card.cardholder.requirements object Yes IssuingCardholderRequirements
card.cardholder.requirements.disabled_reason enum: listed, rejected.listed, requirements.past_due, under_review No If disabled_reason is present, all cards will decline authorizations with cardholder_verification_required reason.
card.cardholder.requirements.past_due array of enum: company.tax_id, individual.card_issuing.user_terms_acceptance.date, individual.card_issuing.user_terms_acceptance.ip, individual.dob.day, individual.dob.month, individual.dob.year, ... No Array of fields that need to be collected in order to verify and re-enable the cardholder.
card.cardholder.spending_controls object No Rules that control spending across this cardholder's cards. Refer to our documentation for more details.
card.cardholder.status enum: active, blocked, inactive Yes Specifies whether to permit authorizations on this cardholder's cards.
card.cardholder.type enum: company, individual Yes One of individual or company. See Choose a cardholder type for more details.
card.created integer (unix-time) Yes Time at which the object was created. Measured in seconds since the Unix epoch.
card.currency string (currency) Yes Three-letter ISO currency code, in lowercase. Supported currencies are usd in the US, eur in the EU, and gbp in the UK.
card.cvc string No The card's CVC. For security reasons, this is only available for virtual cards, and will be omitted unless you explicitly request it with the expand parameter. Additionally, it's only available via the "Retrieve a card" endpoint, not via "List all cards" or any other endpoint.
card.exp_month integer Yes The expiration month of the card.
card.exp_year integer Yes The expiration year of the card.
card.financial_account string No The financial account this card is attached to.
card.id string Yes Unique identifier for the object.
card.last4 string Yes The last 4 digits of the card number.
card.latest_fraud_warning object No Stripe’s assessment of whether this card’s details have been compromised. If this property isn't null, cancel and reissue the card to prevent fraudulent activity risk.
card.lifecycle_controls object No Rules that control the lifecycle of this card, such as automatic cancellation. Refer to our documentation for more details.
card.livemode boolean Yes If the object exists in live mode, the value is true. If the object exists in test mode, the value is false.
card.metadata object Yes Set of key-value pairs that you can attach to an object. This can be useful for storing additional information about the object in a structured format.
card.number string No The full unredacted card number. For security reasons, this is only available for virtual cards, and will be omitted unless you explicitly request it with the expand parameter. Additionally, it's only available via the "Retrieve a card" endpoint, not via "List all cards" or any other endpoint.
card.object enum: issuing.card Yes String representing the object's type. Objects of the same type share the same value.
card.personalization_design string or object No The personalization design object belonging to this card.
card.replaced_by string or object No The latest card that replaces this card, if any.
card.replacement_for string or object No The card this card replaces, if any.
card.replacement_reason enum: damaged, expired, fulfillment_error, lost, stolen No The reason why the previous card needed to be replaced.
card.second_line string No Text separate from cardholder name, printed on the card.
card.shipping object No Where and how the card will be shipped.
card.spending_controls object Yes IssuingCardAuthorizationControls
card.spending_controls.allowed_card_presences array of enum: not_present, present No Array of card presence statuses from which authorizations will be allowed. Possible options are present, not_present. All other statuses will be blocked. Cannot be set with blocked_card_presences. Provide an empty value to unset this control.
card.spending_controls.allowed_categories array of enum: ac_refrigeration_repair, accounting_bookkeeping_services, advertising_services, agricultural_cooperative, airlines_air_carriers, airports_flying_fields, ... No Array of strings containing categories of authorizations to allow. All other categories will be blocked. Cannot be set with blocked_categories.
card.spending_controls.allowed_merchant_countries array of string No Array of strings containing representing countries from which authorizations will be allowed. Authorizations from merchants in all other countries will be declined. Country codes should be ISO 3166 alpha-2 country codes (for example US). Cannot be set with blocked_merchant_countries. Provide an empty value to unset this control.
card.spending_controls.blocked_card_presences array of enum: not_present, present No Array of card presence statuses from which authorizations will be declined. Possible options are present, not_present. Cannot be set with allowed_card_presences. Provide an empty value to unset this control.
card.spending_controls.blocked_categories array of enum: ac_refrigeration_repair, accounting_bookkeeping_services, advertising_services, agricultural_cooperative, airlines_air_carriers, airports_flying_fields, ... No Array of strings containing categories of authorizations to decline. All other categories will be allowed. Cannot be set with allowed_categories.
card.spending_controls.blocked_merchant_countries array of string No Array of strings containing representing countries from which authorizations will be declined. Country codes should be ISO 3166 alpha-2 country codes (for example US). Cannot be set with allowed_merchant_countries. Provide an empty value to unset this control.
card.spending_controls.spending_limits array of object No Limit spending with amount-based rules that apply across any cards this card replaced (that is, its replacement_for card and that card's replacement_for card, up the chain).
card.spending_controls.spending_limits[].amount integer Yes Maximum amount allowed to spend per interval. This amount is in the card's currency and in the smallest currency unit.
card.spending_controls.spending_limits[].categories array of enum: ac_refrigeration_repair, accounting_bookkeeping_services, advertising_services, agricultural_cooperative, airlines_air_carriers, airports_flying_fields, ... No Array of strings containing categories this limit applies to. Omitting this field will apply the limit to all categories.
card.spending_controls.spending_limits[].interval enum: all_time, daily, monthly, per_authorization, weekly, yearly Yes Interval (or event) to which the amount applies.
card.spending_controls.spending_limits_currency string (currency) No Currency of the amounts within spending_limits. Always the same as the currency of the card.
card.status enum: active, canceled, inactive Yes Whether authorizations can be approved on this card. May be blocked from activating cards depending on past-due Cardholder requirements. Defaults to inactive.
card.type enum: physical, virtual Yes The type of the card.
card.wallets object No Information relating to digital wallets (like Apple Pay and Google Pay).
card_presence enum: not_present, present No Whether the card was present at the point of sale for the authorization.
cardholder string or object No The cardholder to whom this authorization belongs.
created integer (unix-time) Yes Time at which the object was created. Measured in seconds since the Unix epoch.
currency string (currency) Yes The currency of the cardholder. This currency can be different from the currency presented at authorization and the merchant_currency field on this authorization. Three-letter ISO currency code, in lowercase. Must be a supported currency.
fleet object No Fleet-specific information for authorizations using Fleet cards.
fraud_challenges array of object No Fraud challenges sent to the cardholder, if this authorization was declined for fraud risk reasons.
fraud_challenges[].channel enum: sms Yes The method by which the fraud challenge was delivered to the cardholder.
fraud_challenges[].status enum: expired, pending, rejected, undeliverable, verified Yes The status of the fraud challenge.
fraud_challenges[].undeliverable_reason enum: no_phone_number, unsupported_phone_number No If the challenge is not deliverable, the reason why.
fuel object No Information about fuel that was purchased with this transaction. Typically this information is received from the merchant after the authorization has been approved and the fuel dispensed.
id string Yes Unique identifier for the object.
livemode boolean Yes If the object exists in live mode, the value is true. If the object exists in test mode, the value is false.
merchant_amount integer Yes The total amount that was authorized or rejected. This amount is in the merchant_currency and in the smallest currency unit. merchant_amount should be the same as amount, unless merchant_currency and currency are different.
merchant_currency string (currency) Yes The local currency that was presented to the cardholder for the authorization. This currency can be different from the cardholder currency and the currency field on this authorization. Three-letter ISO currency code, in lowercase. Must be a supported currency.
merchant_data object Yes IssuingAuthorizationMerchantData
merchant_data.category string Yes A categorization of the seller's type of business. See our merchant categories guide for a list of possible values.
merchant_data.category_code string Yes The merchant category code for the seller’s business
merchant_data.city string No City where the seller is located
merchant_data.country string No Country where the seller is located
merchant_data.name string No Name of the seller
merchant_data.network_id string Yes Identifier assigned to the seller by the card network. Different card networks may assign different network_id fields to the same merchant.
merchant_data.postal_code string No Postal code where the seller is located
merchant_data.state string No State where the seller is located
merchant_data.tax_id string No The seller's tax identification number. Currently populated for French merchants only.
merchant_data.terminal_id string No An ID assigned by the seller to the location of the sale.
merchant_data.url string No URL provided by the merchant on a 3DS request
metadata object Yes Set of key-value pairs that you can attach to an object. This can be useful for storing additional information about the object in a structured format.
network_data object No Details about the authorization, such as identifiers, set by the card network.
object enum: issuing.authorization Yes String representing the object's type. Objects of the same type share the same value.
pending_request object No The pending authorization request. This field will only be non-null during an ``issuing_authorization.request webhook.
request_history array of object Yes History of every time a pending_request authorization was approved/declined, either by you directly or by Stripe (for example based on your spending_controls). If the merchant changes the authorization by performing an incremental authorization, you can look at this field to see the previous requests for the authorization. This field can be helpful in determining why a given authorization was approved/declined.
request_history[].amount integer Yes The ``pending_request.amount at the time of the request, presented in your card's currency and in the smallest currency unit. Stripe held this amount from your account to fund the authorization if the request was approved.
request_history[].amount_details object No Detailed breakdown of amount components. These amounts are denominated in currency and in the smallest currency unit.
request_history[].approved boolean Yes Whether this request was approved.
request_history[].authorization_code string No A code created by Stripe which is shared with the merchant to validate the authorization. This field will be populated if the authorization message was approved. The code typically starts with the letter "S", followed by a six-digit number. For example, "S498162". Please note that the code is not guaranteed to be unique across authorizations.
request_history[].created integer (unix-time) Yes Time at which the object was created. Measured in seconds since the Unix epoch.
request_history[].currency string Yes Three-letter ISO currency code, in lowercase. Must be a supported currency.
request_history[].merchant_amount integer Yes The pending_request`.`merchant_amount at the time of the request, presented in the merchant_currency and in the smallest currency unit.
request_history[].merchant_currency string Yes The currency that was collected by the merchant and presented to the cardholder for the authorization. Three-letter ISO currency code, in lowercase. Must be a supported currency.
request_history[].network_risk_score integer No The card network's estimate of the likelihood that an authorization is fraudulent. Takes on values between 1 and 99.
request_history[].reason enum: account_disabled, card_active, card_canceled, card_expired, card_inactive, cardholder_blocked, ... Yes When an authorization is approved or declined by you or by Stripe, this field provides additional detail on the reason for the outcome.
request_history[].reason_message string No If the request_history`.reason` is webhook_error`` because the direct webhook response is invalid (for example, parsing errors or missing parameters), we surface a more detailed error message via this field.
request_history[].requested_at integer (unix-time) No Time when the card network received an authorization request from the acquirer in UTC. Referred to by networks as transmission time.
status enum: closed, expired, pending, reversed Yes The current status of the authorization in its lifecycle.
token string or object No Token object used for this authorization. If a network token was not used for this authorization, this field will be null.
transactions array of object Yes List of transactions associated with this authorization.
transactions[].amount integer Yes The transaction amount, which will be reflected in your balance. This amount is in your currency and in the smallest currency unit.
transactions[].amount_details object No Detailed breakdown of amount components. These amounts are denominated in currency and in the smallest currency unit.
transactions[].authorization string or object No The Authorization object that led to this transaction.
transactions[].balance_transaction string or object No ID of the balance transaction associated with this transaction.
transactions[].card string or object Yes The card used to make this transaction.
transactions[].cardholder string or object No The cardholder to whom this transaction belongs.
transactions[].created integer (unix-time) Yes Time at which the object was created. Measured in seconds since the Unix epoch.
transactions[].currency string (currency) Yes Three-letter ISO currency code, in lowercase. Must be a supported currency.
transactions[].dispute string or object No If you've disputed the transaction, the ID of the dispute.
transactions[].id string Yes Unique identifier for the object.
transactions[].livemode boolean Yes If the object exists in live mode, the value is true. If the object exists in test mode, the value is false.
transactions[].merchant_amount integer Yes The amount that the merchant will receive, denominated in merchant_currency and in the smallest currency unit. It will be different from amount if the merchant is taking payment in a different currency.
transactions[].merchant_currency string (currency) Yes The currency with which the merchant is taking payment.
transactions[].merchant_data object Yes IssuingAuthorizationMerchantData
transactions[].merchant_data.category string Yes A categorization of the seller's type of business. See our merchant categories guide for a list of possible values.
transactions[].merchant_data.category_code string Yes The merchant category code for the seller’s business
transactions[].merchant_data.city string No City where the seller is located
transactions[].merchant_data.country string No Country where the seller is located
transactions[].merchant_data.name string No Name of the seller
transactions[].merchant_data.network_id string Yes Identifier assigned to the seller by the card network. Different card networks may assign different network_id fields to the same merchant.
transactions[].merchant_data.postal_code string No Postal code where the seller is located
transactions[].merchant_data.state string No State where the seller is located
transactions[].merchant_data.tax_id string No The seller's tax identification number. Currently populated for French merchants only.
transactions[].merchant_data.terminal_id string No An ID assigned by the seller to the location of the sale.
transactions[].merchant_data.url string No URL provided by the merchant on a 3DS request
transactions[].metadata object Yes Set of key-value pairs that you can attach to an object. This can be useful for storing additional information about the object in a structured format.
transactions[].network_data object No Details about the transaction, such as processing dates, set by the card network.
transactions[].object enum: issuing.transaction Yes String representing the object's type. Objects of the same type share the same value.
transactions[].purchase_details object No Additional purchase information that is optionally provided by the merchant.
transactions[].token string or object No Token object used for this transaction. If a network token was not used for this transaction, this field will be null.
transactions[].treasury object No Treasury details related to this transaction if it was created on a [FinancialAccount](/docs/api/treasury/financial_accounts
transactions[].type enum: capture, refund Yes The nature of the transaction.
transactions[].wallet enum: apple_pay, google_pay, samsung_pay No The digital wallet used for this transaction. One of apple_pay, google_pay, or samsung_pay.
treasury object No Treasury details related to this authorization if it was created on a FinancialAccount.
verification_data object Yes IssuingAuthorizationVerificationData
verification_data.address_line1_check enum: match, mismatch, not_provided Yes Whether the cardholder provided an address first line and if it matched the cardholder’s billing.address.line1.
verification_data.address_postal_code_check enum: match, mismatch, not_provided Yes Whether the cardholder provided a postal code and if it matched the cardholder’s billing.address.postal_code``.
verification_data.authentication_exemption object No The exemption applied to this authorization.
verification_data.cvc_check enum: match, mismatch, not_provided Yes Whether the cardholder provided a CVC and if it matched Stripe’s record.

wallet string No The digital wallet used for this transaction. One of apple_pay, google_pay, or samsung_pay. Will populate as null when no digital wallet was utilized.
default: Error response

error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API

POST /v1/issuing/authorizations/{authorization}/approve

Operation ID: PostIssuingAuthorizationsAuthorizationApprove.

Authentication: basic token (basicAuth) or bearer token (bearerAuth).

Parameters

| Parameter | In | Type | Required | Description | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | authorization | path | string | Yes | |

Request body

application/x-www-form-urlencoded

Property Type Required Description
amount integer No If the authorization's pending_request`.`is_amount_controllable property is true, you may provide this value to control how much to hold for the authorization. Must be positive (use decline to decline an authorization request).
expand array of string No Specifies which fields in the response should be expanded.
metadata object or enum: No Set of key-value pairs that you can attach to an object. This can be useful for storing additional information about the object in a structured format. Individual keys can be unset by posting an empty value to them. All keys can be unset by posting an empty value to metadata.

Responses

200: Successful response

For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API

A PaymentIntent transitions through multiple statuses throughout its lifetime as it interfaces with Stripe.js to perform authentication flows and ultimately creates at most one successful charge. The minimum amount is $0.50 US or equivalent in charge currency. The amount value supports up to eight digits (for example, a value of 99999999 for a USD charge of $999,999.99).

error.payment_intent.review string or object No ID of the review associated with this PaymentIntent, if any.
error.payment_intent.setup_future_usage enum: off_session, on_session No Indicates that you intend to make future payments with this PaymentIntent's payment method. If you provide a Customer with the PaymentIntent, you can use this parameter to attach the payment method to the Customer after the PaymentIntent is confirmed and the customer completes any required actions. If you don't provide a Customer, you can still attach the payment method to a Customer after the transaction completes. If the payment method is card_present and isn't a digital wallet, Stripe creates and attaches a generated_card payment method representing the card to the Customer instead. When processing card payments, Stripe uses setup_future_usage to help you comply with regional legislation and network rules, such as SCA.
error.payment_intent.shipping object No Shipping information for this PaymentIntent.
error.payment_intent.statement_descriptor string No Text that appears on the customer's statement as the statement descriptor for a non-card charge. This value overrides the account's default statement descriptor. For information about requirements, including the 22-character limit, see the Statement Descriptor docs. Setting this value for a card charge returns an error. For card charges, set the statement_descriptor_suffix instead.
error.payment_intent.statement_descriptor_suffix string No Provides information about a card charge. Concatenated to the account's statement descriptor prefix to form the complete statement descriptor that appears on the customer's statement.
error.payment_intent.status enum: canceled, processing, requires_action, requires_capture, requires_confirmation, requires_payment_method, ... Yes Status of this PaymentIntent, one of requires_payment_method, requires_confirmation, requires_action, processing, requires_capture, canceled, or succeeded. Read more about each PaymentIntent status.
error.payment_intent.transfer_data object No The data that automatically creates a Transfer after the payment finalizes. Learn more about the use case for connected accounts.
error.payment_intent.transfer_group string No A string that identifies the resulting payment as part of a group. Learn more about the use case for connected accounts.
error.payment_method object No PaymentMethod objects represent your customer's payment instruments. You can use them with PaymentIntents to collect payments or save them to Customer objects to store instrument details for future payments. Related guides: Payment Methods and More Payment Scenarios.
error.payment_method_type string No If the error is specific to the type of payment method, the payment method type that had a problem. This field is only populated for invoice-related errors.
error.request_log_url string No A URL to the request log entry in your dashboard.
error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API
error.setup_intent.allowed_payment_method_types array of enum: acss_debit, affirm, afterpay_clearpay, alipay, alma, amazon_pay, ... No The list of payment method types to allow for this SetupIntent. Stripe will only use methods in this list when determining the payment methods to offer.
error.setup_intent.application string or object No ID of the Connect application that created the SetupIntent.
error.setup_intent.attach_to_self boolean No If present, the SetupIntent's payment method will be attached to the in-context Stripe Account. It can only be used for this Stripe Account’s own money movement flows like InboundTransfer and OutboundTransfers. It cannot be set to true when setting up a PaymentMethod for a Customer, and defaults to false when attaching a PaymentMethod to a Customer.
error.setup_intent.automatic_payment_methods object No Settings for dynamic payment methods compatible with this Setup Intent
error.setup_intent.cancellation_reason enum: abandoned, duplicate, requested_by_customer No Reason for cancellation of this SetupIntent, one of abandoned, requested_by_customer, or duplicate.
error.setup_intent.client_secret string No The client secret of this SetupIntent. Used for client-side retrieval using a publishable key. The client secret can be used to complete payment setup from your frontend. It should not be stored, logged, or exposed to anyone other than the customer. Make sure that you have TLS enabled on any page that includes the client secret.
error.setup_intent.created integer (unix-time) Yes Time at which the object was created. Measured in seconds since the Unix epoch.
error.setup_intent.customer string or object or object No ID of the Customer this SetupIntent belongs to, if one exists. If present, the SetupIntent's payment method will be attached to the Customer on successful setup. Payment methods attached to other Customers cannot be used with this SetupIntent.
error.setup_intent.customer_account string No ID of the Account this SetupIntent belongs to, if one exists. If present, the SetupIntent's payment method will be attached to the Account on successful setup. Payment methods attached to other Accounts cannot be used with this SetupIntent.
error.setup_intent.description string No An arbitrary string attached to the object. Often useful for displaying to users.

error.setup_intent.flow_directions array of enum: inbound, outbound No Indicates the directions of money movement for which this payment method is intended to be used. Include inbound if you intend to use the payment method as the origin to pull funds from. Include outbound if you intend to use the payment method as the destination to send funds to. You can include both if you intend to use the payment method for both purposes.

error.setup_intent.on_behalf_of string or object No The account (if any) for which the setup is intended.

error.setup_intent.usage string Yes Indicates how the payment method is intended to be used in the future. Use on_session if you intend to only reuse the payment method when the customer is in your checkout flow. Use off_session if your customer may or may not be in your checkout flow. If not provided, this value defaults to off_session.
error.source object or object or object No The source object for errors returned on a request involving a source.
error.type enum: api_error, card_error, idempotency_error, invalid_request_error Yes The type of error returned. One of api_error, card_error, idempotency_error, or invalid_request_error

POST /v1/issuing/authorizations/{authorization}/decline

Operation ID: PostIssuingAuthorizationsAuthorizationDecline.

Authentication: basic token (basicAuth) or bearer token (bearerAuth).

Parameters

| Parameter | In | Type | Required | Description | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | authorization | path | string | Yes | |

Request body

application/x-www-form-urlencoded

Property Type Required Description
expand array of string No Specifies which fields in the response should be expanded.
metadata object or enum: No Set of key-value pairs that you can attach to an object. This can be useful for storing additional information about the object in a structured format. Individual keys can be unset by posting an empty value to them. All keys can be unset by posting an empty value to metadata.

Responses

200: Successful response

error.payment_intent object No A PaymentIntent guides you through the process of collecting a payment from your customer. We recommend that you create exactly one PaymentIntent for each order or customer session in your system. You can reference the PaymentIntent later to see the history of payment attempts for a particular session. A PaymentIntent transitions through multiple statuses throughout its lifetime as it interfaces with Stripe.js to perform authentication flows and ultimately creates at most one successful charge. Related guide: Payment Intents API

No The list of payment method types allowed for use with this payment. Stripe automatically returns compatible payment methods from this list in the payment_method_types field of the response, based on the other PaymentIntent parameters, such as currency, amount, and customer.
error.payment_intent.amount integer No Amount intended to be collected by this PaymentIntent. A positive integer representing how much to charge in the smallest currency unit (for example, 100 cents to charge $1.00 or 100 to charge ¥100, a zero-decimal currency). The minimum amount is $0.50 US or equivalent in charge currency. The amount value supports up to eight digits (for example, a value of 99999999 for a USD charge of $999,999.99).

error.payment_intent.setup_future_usage enum: off_session, on_session No Indicates that you intend to make future payments with this PaymentIntent's payment method. If you provide a Customer with the PaymentIntent, you can use this parameter to attach the payment method to the Customer after the PaymentIntent is confirmed and the customer completes any required actions. If you don't provide a Customer, you can still attach the payment method to a Customer after the transaction completes. If the payment method is card_present and isn't a digital wallet, Stripe creates and attaches a generated_card payment method representing the card to the Customer instead. When processing card payments, Stripe uses setup_future_usage to help you comply with regional legislation and network rules, such as SCA.

error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API
error.setup_intent.allowed_payment_method_types array of enum: acss_debit, affirm, afterpay_clearpay, alipay, alma, amazon_pay, ... No The list of payment method types to allow for this SetupIntent. Stripe will only use methods in this list when determining the payment methods to offer.
error.setup_intent.application string or object No ID of the Connect application that created the SetupIntent.
error.setup_intent.attach_to_self boolean No If present, the SetupIntent's payment method will be attached to the in-context Stripe Account. It can only be used for this Stripe Account’s own money movement flows like InboundTransfer and OutboundTransfers. It cannot be set to true when setting up a PaymentMethod for a Customer, and defaults to false when attaching a PaymentMethod to a Customer.

POST /v1/test_helpers/issuing/authorizations/{authorization}/expire

Operation ID: PostTestHelpersIssuingAuthorizationsAuthorizationExpire.

Authentication: basic token (basicAuth) or bearer token (bearerAuth).

| Parameter | In | Type | Required | Description | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | authorization | path | string | Yes | |

application/x-www-form-urlencoded

Property Type Required Description

Responses

200: Successful response

If the merchant changes the authorization by performing an incremental authorization, you can look at this field to see the previous requests for the authorization. This field can be helpful in determining why a given authorization was approved/declined.
request_history[].amount integer Yes The ``pending_request.amount at the time of the request, presented in your card's currency and in the smallest currency unit. Stripe held this amount from your account to fund the authorization if the request was approved.
request_history[].amount_details object No Detailed breakdown of amount components. These amounts are denominated in currency and in the smallest currency unit.
request_history[].approved boolean Yes Whether this request was approved.
request_history[].authorization_code string No A code created by Stripe which is shared with the merchant to validate the authorization. This field will be populated if the authorization message was approved. The code typically starts with the letter "S", followed by a six-digit number. For example, "S498162". Please note that the code is not guaranteed to be unique across authorizations.
request_history[].created integer (unix-time) Yes Time at which the object was created. Measured in seconds since the Unix epoch.

request_history[].network_risk_score integer No The card network's estimate of the likelihood that an authorization is fraudulent. Takes on values between 1 and 99.

For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API

error.code string No For some errors that could be handled programmatically, a short string indicating the error code reported.

error.payment_intent object No A PaymentIntent guides you through the process of collecting a payment from your customer. We recommend that you create exactly one PaymentIntent for each order or customer session in your system. You can reference the PaymentIntent later to see the history of payment attempts for a particular session. A PaymentIntent transitions through multiple statuses throughout its lifetime as it interfaces with Stripe.js to perform authentication flows and ultimately creates at most one successful charge. Related guide: Payment Intents API
error.payment_intent.allowed_payment_method_types array of enum: acss_debit, affirm, afterpay_clearpay, alipay, alma, amazon_pay, ... No The list of payment method types allowed for use with this payment. Stripe automatically returns compatible payment methods from this list in the payment_method_types field of the response, based on the other PaymentIntent parameters, such as currency, amount, and customer.
error.payment_intent.amount integer No Amount intended to be collected by this PaymentIntent. A positive integer representing how much to charge in the smallest currency unit (for example, 100 cents to charge $1.00 or 100 to charge ¥100, a zero-decimal currency). The minimum amount is $0.50 US or equivalent in charge currency. The amount value supports up to eight digits (for example, a value of 99999999 for a USD charge of $999,999.99).

error.payment_intent.setup_future_usage enum: off_session, on_session No Indicates that you intend to make future payments with this PaymentIntent's payment method. If you provide a Customer with the PaymentIntent, you can use this parameter to attach the payment method to the Customer after the PaymentIntent is confirmed and the customer completes any required actions. If you don't provide a Customer, you can still attach the payment method to a Customer after the transaction completes. If the payment method is card_present and isn't a digital wallet, Stripe creates and attaches a generated_card payment method representing the card to the Customer instead. When processing card payments, Stripe uses setup_future_usage to help you comply with regional legislation and network rules, such as SCA.

Setting this value for a card charge returns an error. For card charges, set the statement_descriptor_suffix instead.

error.payment_method object No PaymentMethod objects represent your customer's payment instruments. You can use them with PaymentIntents to collect payments or save them to Customer objects to store instrument details for future payments. Related guides: Payment Methods and More Payment Scenarios.

error.request_log_url string No A URL to the request log entry in your dashboard.
error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API
error.setup_intent.allowed_payment_method_types array of enum: acss_debit, affirm, afterpay_clearpay, alipay, alma, amazon_pay, ... No The list of payment method types to allow for this SetupIntent. Stripe will only use methods in this list when determining the payment methods to offer.
error.setup_intent.application string or object No ID of the Connect application that created the SetupIntent.
error.setup_intent.attach_to_self boolean No If present, the SetupIntent's payment method will be attached to the in-context Stripe Account. It can only be used for this Stripe Account’s own money movement flows like InboundTransfer and OutboundTransfers. It cannot be set to true when setting up a PaymentMethod for a Customer, and defaults to false when attaching a PaymentMethod to a Customer.

The client secret can be used to complete payment setup from your frontend. It should not be stored, logged, or exposed to anyone other than the customer. Make sure that you have TLS enabled on any page that includes the client secret.

Include inbound if you intend to use the payment method as the origin to pull funds from. Include outbound if you intend to use the payment method as the destination to send funds to. Use on_session if you intend to only reuse the payment method when the customer is in your checkout flow. Use off_session if your customer may or may not be in your checkout flow. If not provided, this value defaults to off_session.

POST /v1/test_helpers/issuing/authorizations/{authorization}/fraud_challenges/respond

Operation ID: PostTestHelpersIssuingAuthorizationsAuthorizationFraudChallengesRespond.

Authentication: basic token (basicAuth) or bearer token (bearerAuth).

| Parameter | In | Type | Required | Description | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | authorization | path | string | Yes | |

error.payment_intent.amount integer No Amount intended to be collected by this PaymentIntent. A positive integer representing how much to charge in the smallest currency unit (for example, 100 cents to charge $1.00 or 100 to charge ¥100, a zero-decimal currency). The minimum amount is $0.50 US or equivalent in charge currency. The amount value supports up to eight digits (for example, a value of 99999999 for a USD charge of $999,999.99).

error.payment_intent.client_secret string No The client secret of this PaymentIntent. Used for client-side retrieval using a publishable key. The client secret can be used to complete a payment from your frontend. It should not be stored, logged, or exposed to anyone other than the customer. error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API

POST /v1/test_helpers/issuing/authorizations/{authorization}/increment

Operation ID: PostTestHelpersIssuingAuthorizationsAuthorizationIncrement.

Authentication: basic token (basicAuth) or bearer token (bearerAuth).

Parameters

| Parameter | In | Type | Required | Description | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | authorization | path | string | Yes | |

Request body

application/x-www-form-urlencoded

Property Type Required Description
expand array of string No Specifies which fields in the response should be expanded.
increment_amount integer Yes The amount to increment the authorization by. This amount is in the authorization currency and in the smallest currency unit.
is_amount_controllable boolean No If set true, you may provide amount to control how much to hold for the authorization.

Responses

200: Successful response

application/json

error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API

POST /v1/test_helpers/issuing/authorizations/{authorization}/reverse

Operation ID: PostTestHelpersIssuingAuthorizationsAuthorizationReverse.

Authentication: basic token (basicAuth) or bearer token (bearerAuth).

Parameters

| Parameter | In | Type | Required | Description | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | authorization | path | string | Yes | |

Request body

application/x-www-form-urlencoded

Property Type Required Description
expand array of string No Specifies which fields in the response should be expanded.
reverse_amount integer No The amount to reverse from the authorization. If not provided, the full amount of the authorization will be reversed. This amount is in the authorization currency and in the smallest currency unit.

Responses

200: Successful response

No Array of strings containing categories of authorizations to decline. All other categories will be allowed. Cannot be set with allowed_categories.

Country codes should be ISO 3166 alpha-2 country codes (for example US). Cannot be set with allowed_merchant_countries. Provide an empty value to unset this control.

error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API

error.param string No If the error is parameter-specific, the parameter related to the error. For example, you can use this to display a message near the correct form field.
error.payment_intent object No A PaymentIntent guides you through the process of collecting a payment from your customer. We recommend that you create exactly one PaymentIntent for each order or customer session in your system. You can reference the PaymentIntent later to see the history of payment attempts for a particular session. A PaymentIntent transitions through multiple statuses throughout its lifetime as it interfaces with Stripe.js to perform authentication flows and ultimately creates at most one successful charge. Related guide: Payment Intents API
error.payment_intent.allowed_payment_method_types array of enum: acss_debit, affirm, afterpay_clearpay, alipay, alma, amazon_pay, ... No The list of payment method types allowed for use with this payment. Stripe automatically returns compatible payment methods from this list in the payment_method_types field of the response, based on the other PaymentIntent parameters, such as currency, amount, and customer.
error.payment_intent.amount integer No Amount intended to be collected by this PaymentIntent. A positive integer representing how much to charge in the smallest currency unit (for example, 100 cents to charge $1.00 or 100 to charge ¥100, a zero-decimal currency). The minimum amount is $0.50 US or equivalent in charge currency. The amount value supports up to eight digits (for example, a value of 99999999 for a USD charge of $999,999.99).
error.payment_intent.amount_capturable integer No Amount that can be captured from this PaymentIntent.
error.payment_intent.amount_details object or object No
error.payment_intent.amount_received integer No Amount that this PaymentIntent collects.
error.payment_intent.application string or object No ID of the Connect application that created the PaymentIntent.
error.payment_intent.application_fee_amount integer No The amount of the application fee (if any) that will be requested to be applied to the payment and transferred to the application owner's Stripe account. The amount of the application fee collected will be capped at the total amount captured. For more information, see the PaymentIntents use case for connected accounts.
error.payment_intent.automatic_payment_methods object No Settings to configure compatible payment methods from the Stripe Dashboard
error.payment_intent.canceled_at integer (unix-time) No Populated when status is canceled, this is the time at which the PaymentIntent was canceled. Measured in seconds since the Unix epoch.
error.payment_intent.cancellation_reason enum: abandoned, automatic, duplicate, expired, failed_invoice, fraudulent, ... No Reason for cancellation of this PaymentIntent, either user-provided (duplicate, fraudulent, requested_by_customer, or abandoned) or generated by Stripe internally (failed_invoice, void_invoice, automatic, or expired).

If the payment method is card_present and isn't a digital wallet, Stripe creates and attaches a generated_card payment method representing the card to the Customer instead. When processing card payments, Stripe uses setup_future_usage to help you comply with regional legislation and network rules, such as SCA.

error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API

list

GET /v1/issuing/authorizations

Operation ID: GetIssuingAuthorizations.

Authentication: basic token (basicAuth) or bearer token (bearerAuth).

Parameters

| Parameter | In | Type | Required | Description | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | card | query | string | No | Only return authorizations that belong to the given card. | | cardholder | query | string | No | Only return authorizations that belong to the given cardholder. | | created | query | object or integer | No | Only return authorizations that were created during the given date interval. | | ending_before | query | string | No | A cursor for use in pagination. ending_before is an object ID that defines your place in the list. For instance, if you make a list request and receive 100 objects, starting with obj_bar, your subsequent call can include ending_before`=`obj_bar in order to fetch the previous page of the list. | | expand | query | array of string | No | Specifies which fields in the response should be expanded. | | limit | query | integer | No | A limit on the number of objects to be returned. Limit can range between 1 and 100, and the default is 10. | | starting_after | query | string | No | A cursor for use in pagination. starting_after is an object ID that defines your place in the list. For instance, if you make a list request and receive 100 objects, ending with obj_foo, your subsequent call can include starting_after`=`obj_foo in order to fetch the next page of the list. | | status | query | enum: closed, expired, pending, reversed | No | Only return authorizations with the given status. One of pending, closed, or reversed. |

Request body

application/x-www-form-urlencoded

The request body is object.

Responses

200: Successful response

error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API

load

GET /v1/issuing/authorizations/{authorization}

Operation ID: GetIssuingAuthorizationsAuthorization.

Authentication: basic token (basicAuth) or bearer token (bearerAuth).

Parameters

| Parameter | In | Type | Required | Description | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | authorization | path | string | Yes | | | expand | query | array of string | No | Specifies which fields in the response should be expanded. |

Request body

application/x-www-form-urlencoded

The request body is object.

Responses

200: Successful response

application/json

Property Type Required Description

A PaymentIntent transitions through multiple statuses throughout its lifetime as it interfaces with Stripe.js to perform authentication flows and ultimately creates at most one successful charge. The minimum amount is $0.50 US or equivalent in charge currency. The amount value supports up to eight digits (for example, a value of 99999999 for a USD charge of $999,999.99).

error.setup_intent object No A SetupIntent guides you through the process of setting up and saving a customer's payment credentials for future payments. For example, you can use a SetupIntent to set up and save your customer's card without immediately collecting a payment. Later, you can use PaymentIntents to drive the payment flow. Create a SetupIntent when you're ready to collect your customer's payment credentials. Don't maintain long-lived, unconfirmed SetupIntents because they might not be valid. The SetupIntent transitions through multiple statuses as it guides you through the setup process. Successful SetupIntents result in payment credentials that are optimized for future payments. For example, cardholders in certain regions might need to be run through Strong Customer Authentication during payment method collection to streamline later off-session payments. If you use the SetupIntent with a Customer, it automatically attaches the resulting payment method to that Customer after successful setup. We recommend using SetupIntents or setup_future_usage on PaymentIntents to save payment methods to prevent saving invalid or unoptimized payment methods. By using SetupIntents, you can reduce friction for your customers, even as regulations change over time. Related guide: Setup Intents API

You can include both if you intend to use the payment method for both purposes.
error.setup_intent.id string Yes Unique identifier for the object.
error.setup_intent.last_setup_error object No The error encountered in the previous SetupIntent confirmation.
error.setup_intent.latest_attempt string or object No The most recent SetupAttempt for this SetupIntent.
error.setup_intent.livemode boolean Yes If the object exists in live mode, the value is true. If the object exists in test mode, the value is false.
error.setup_intent.managed_payments object No
error.setup_intent.mandate string or object No ID of the multi use Mandate generated by the SetupIntent.
error.setup_intent.metadata object No Set of key-value pairs that you can attach to an object. This can be useful for storing additional information about the object in a structured format.
error.setup_intent.next_action object No If present, this property tells you what actions you need to take in order for your customer to continue payment setup.
error.setup_intent.object enum: setup_intent Yes String representing the object's type. Objects of the same type share the same value.
error.setup_intent.on_behalf_of string or object No The account (if any) for which the setup is intended.
error.setup_intent.payment_method string or object No ID of the payment method used with this SetupIntent. If the payment method is card_present and isn't a digital wallet, then the generated_card associated with the latest_attempt is attached to the Customer instead.
error.setup_intent.payment_method_configuration_details object No Information about the payment method configuration used for this Setup Intent.
error.setup_intent.payment_method_options object No Payment method-specific configuration for this SetupIntent.
error.setup_intent.payment_method_types array of string Yes The list of payment method types (for example card) that this SetupIntent is allowed to set up. A list of valid payment method types can be found here.
error.setup_intent.single_use_mandate string or object No ID of the single_use Mandate generated by the SetupIntent.
error.setup_intent.status enum: canceled, processing, requires_action, requires_confirmation, requires_payment_method, succeeded Yes Status of this SetupIntent, one of requires_payment_method, requires_confirmation, requires_action, processing, canceled, or succeeded.
error.setup_intent.usage string Yes Indicates how the payment method is intended to be used in the future. Use on_session if you intend to only reuse the payment method when the customer is in your checkout flow. Use off_session if your customer may or may not be in your checkout flow. If not provided, this value defaults to off_session.
error.source object or object or object No The source object for errors returned on a request involving a source.
error.type enum: api_error, card_error, idempotency_error, invalid_request_error Yes The type of error returned. One of api_error, card_error, idempotency_error, or invalid_request_error
